Um... I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about the weight, honestly. 

I mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything, but since I got promoted and I'm up front at meetings more, I started wearing these more fitted work clothes. And... well, I just catch myself judging my whole day by whether my pants feel tight in the morning, or whether I think people can see my stomach when I'm standing at the front of the room. 

It's just really important to me to look professional and capable, and I worry that if I gain weight, people won't take me as seriously. So when I eat too much, I just panic about how it's going to affect my body. 

I know that probably sounds a bit silly, worrying so much about how I look at a construction site... but it's just hard not to fixate on it. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as too superficial...

I hope that's helpful... is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these evenings because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I am usually by myself when it happens, just because it's so late when I finally get back from the site or a meeting. But... no, I don't think I'm doing it on purpose to hide, if that makes sense? \n\nI mean, I wouldn't say I'm exactly proud of eating half a box of cereal in one sitting, but it's really more just that there's nobody else around at that hour anyway. If someone else was in the kitchen, I'd probably still be eating. \n\nI just happen to be alone. I hope I'm answering that right... is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, just out of habit or emotion?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, usually when I get home, I'm genuinely pretty hungry because I've been running around the site all day. \n\nI guess sometimes I might eat a little bit more just because it tastes good or because I'm trying to unwind after dealing with contractors all day. But I wouldn't say I'm eating when I'm completely full or just out of nowhere, you know? \n\nIt really feels like it's mostly just regular hunger catching up with me. I hope that's an okay answer... does that sound pretty normal to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel upset, guilty, or distressed about these eating episodes, or has it started to affect your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it hasn't really affected my job or anything. I mean, I'm still showing up every day, getting everything done on site, keeping the crews on schedule. I'm definitely still functioning totally fine. \n\nI guess... maybe lately I've been turning down some of the after-hours stuff? Like if the guys want to grab a bite after a late meeting, I might just make an excuse and rush home instead. I just... I worry a little bit that if I stay out, the evening might sort of get away from me, and I won't get my routine in, if that makes sense? \n\nBut it's not a huge deal, and I don't feel terrible about it or anything. It's just a little preference I've developed. I hope that's the right kind of answer... is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: About how often do these evenings where you end up eating more than you intended happen? Is it something that happens almost every day, a few times a week, or more like once a week or less?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not that often, honestly. I'd say... maybe about once a week, on average? \n\nI mean, looking back over the last few months, there might be a week where it happens twice, like if a project is really behind schedule and I'm super stressed. But then there are other weeks where it doesn't happen at all. So it kind of just balances out to around once a week for the... you know, the eating and then getting rid of it. \n\nIt's really not an everyday thing at all. I hope that makes sense... does that sound pretty typical for someone in my situation?\n\nDoctor: After these evenings where you eat more than you intended, do you ever try to compensate for it in any way, like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do sometimes try to... fix it? Just a little bit. \n\nI don't do anything crazy like taking pills or running a marathon in the middle of the night. You have to maintain a certain image. And I just get so scared of undoing all that effort. Um... yeah, it's pretty much always tied to those times when I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I don't just do it out of nowhere, I promise. It's only when I get that really full, uncomfortable feeling and start panicking about my clothes fitting the next day.

Um... yeah, I guess it is. I mean, it's usually after a really long day on site, or sometimes if I've had to sit through a stressful neighborhood meeting. I'll get home and just... I don't know, I just start eating.

I still find myself going back to the kitchen for more anyway. \n\nIt's probably just a bad habit from being so exhausted, right? I'm sure a lot of people do that. I hope I'm explaining that okay... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more rapidly than normal?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually just sit at the kitchen counter and eat at a pretty normal pace, I think. Maybe a little fast if I missed lunch during a pour or something, but... no, I wouldn't say it's much more rapid than anyone else. \n\nI haven't really noticed myself rushing through it, anyway. I hope that's helpful... is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these evenings because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I am usually by myself when it happens, just because it's so late when I finally get back from the site or a meeting. But... no, I don't think I'm doing it on purpose to hide, if that makes sense? \n\nI mean, I wouldn't say I'm exactly proud of eating half a box of cereal in one sitting, but it's really more just that there's nobody else around at that hour anyway. If someone else was in the kitchen, I'd probably still be eating. \n\nI just happen to be alone. I hope I'm answering that right... is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, just out of habit or emotion?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, usually when I get home, I'm genuinely pretty hungry because I've been running around the site all day. \n\nI guess sometimes I might eat a little bit more just because it tastes good or because I'm trying to unwind after dealing with contractors all day. But I wouldn't say I'm eating when I'm completely full or just out of nowhere, you know? \n\nIt really feels like it's mostly just regular hunger catching up with me. I hope that's an okay answer... does that sound pretty normal to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel upset, guilty, or distressed about these eating episodes, or has it started to affect your work, relationships, or daily life in any way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it hasn't really affected my job or anything. I mean, I'm still showing up every day, getting everything done on site, keeping the crews on schedule. I'm definitely still functioning totally fine. \n\nI guess... maybe lately I've been turning down some of the after-hours stuff? Like if the guys want to grab a bite after a late meeting, I might just make an excuse and rush home instead. I just... I worry a little bit that if I stay out, the evening might sort of get away from me, and I won't get my routine in, if that makes sense? \n\nBut it's not a huge deal, and I don't feel terrible about it or anything. It's just a little preference I've developed. I hope that's the right kind of answer... is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: About how often do these evenings where you end up eating more than you intended happen? Is it something that happens almost every day, a few times a week, or more like once a week or less?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not that often, honestly. I'd say... maybe about once a week, on average? \n\nI mean, looking back over the last few months, there might be a week where it happens twice, like if a project is really behind schedule and I'm super stressed. But then there are other weeks where it doesn't happen at all. So it kind of just balances out to around once a week for the... you know, the eating and then getting rid of it. \n\nIt's really not an everyday thing at all. I hope that makes sense... does that sound pretty typical for someone in my situation?\n\nDoctor: After these evenings where you eat more than you intended, do you ever try to compensate for it in any way, like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do sometimes try to... fix it? Just a little bit. \n\nI don't do anything crazy like taking pills or running a marathon in the middle of the night. But... maybe a few times a month, if I've had one of those nights where I just ate too much and I start feeling really panicked about it... I might go into the bathroom and just... make myself sick. \n\nIt's just that I work really hard to look put-together and professional on site, especially being a woman in construction, you know? You have to maintain a certain image. And I just get so scared of undoing all that effort. But it's really only a few times a month, so it's not a huge habit or anything. \n\nI know it probably doesn't sound great, but... I'm trying to be honest. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I hope I'm not sounding too messed up...\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself sick after eating, is it specifically because you feel you've eaten too much, or is it more about being worried about gaining weight or how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about the weight, honestly. \n\nI mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything, but since I got promoted and I'm up front at meetings more, I started wearing these more fitted work clothes. And... well, I just catch myself judging my whole day by whether my pants feel tight in the morning, or whether I think people can see my stomach when I'm standing at the front of the room. \n\nIt's just really important to me to look professional and capable, and I worry that if I gain weight, people won't take me as seriously. So when I eat too much, I just panic about how it's going to affect my body. \n\nI know that probably sounds a bit silly, worrying so much about how I look at a construction site... but it's just hard not to fixate on it. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as too superficial...\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself sick in secret, and about how many times has that happened in the past month?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, definitely in private. I would never... I mean, I'd be absolutely mortified if anyone knew. I always make sure I'm completely alone. I'd hate to make anyone uncomfortable or have them think less of me, you know? \n\nAnd as for how many times it happened this past month... gosh, I really don't know an exact number. I don't keep track of it, honestly. Like I mentioned, it's really infrequent, so I just haven't paid attention to a specific count. I just don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything, because I'm really not.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times you’ve made yourself sick in the past month?\n\nPatient: Um... well, if I really sit and think about it... It sounds a bit worse when I say it out loud, and I really want to assure you that it's not an everyday thing. Once a week isn't too terrible, right? I hope that's a better estimate for you... is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself sick, does it usually happen after one of those evenings where you feel you ate too much, and how often would you say that happens in a typical week?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, it's pretty much always tied to those times when I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I don't just do it out of nowhere, I promise. It's only when I get that really full, uncomfortable feeling and start panicking about my clothes fitting the next day. \n\nAnd as for how often... well, like I was saying before, if I look back over the last few months, that whole pattern—the eating too much and then making myself sick—it really only averages out to about once a week. \n\nSome weeks, if work is just crazy and I'm stressed out, it might happen twice. But then I'll have a good week where it doesn't happen at all. So it's not a constant, everyday thing. That's not too extreme, is it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture without making it sound worse than it actually is. Does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself sick, is it usually after a night where you feel like you ate a very large amount of food in one sitting, more than most people would eat in that same time?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it is. I mean, it's usually after a really long day on site, or sometimes if I've had to sit through a stressful neighborhood meeting. I'll get home and just... I don't know, I just start eating. \n\nI'll have some leftover takeout, and then maybe a bowl of cereal, and then some chips... just whatever is around, really. And before I know it, I've eaten way more than I ever intended to, and I just feel so uncomfortably full. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yes, exactly. Like I was mentioning just a bit ago, if I really look back over the last few months, that whole pattern of overeating and then... making myself sick... it really has averaged out to about weekly. 

Even if some weeks it happens twice because work is just overwhelming, there are other weeks where it doesn't happen at all, so it balances out. So, yes, I'd say about once a week is a fair average for a typical week. 

I hope it's okay that I'm repeating myself a little bit here. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions exactly how you need me to, and that I'm being as accurate as possible for you. Does that give you the information you needed?

I still find myself going back to the kitchen for more anyway. \n\nIt's probably just a bad habit from being so exhausted, right? I'm sure a lot of people do that. I hope that's helpful... is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these evenings because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I am usually by myself when it happens, just because it's so late when I finally get back from the site or a meeting. If someone else was in the kitchen, I'd probably still be eating. \n\nI just happen to be alone. Like if the guys want to grab a bite after a late meeting, I might just make an excuse and rush home instead. I just... I worry a little bit that if I stay out, the evening might sort of get away from me, and I won't get my routine in, if that makes sense? \n\nBut it's not a huge deal, and I don't feel terrible about it or anything. I hope that's the right kind of answer... is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: About how often do these evenings where you end up eating more than you intended happen? So it kind of just balances out to around once a week for the... you know, the eating and then getting rid of it. \n\nIt's really not an everyday thing at all. I hope that makes sense... does that sound pretty typical for someone in my situation?\n\nDoctor: After these evenings where you eat more than you intended, do you ever try to compensate for it in any way, like making yourself sick, using laxatives, or doing extra exercise?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I do sometimes try to... fix it? Just a little bit. \n\nI don't do anything crazy like taking pills or running a marathon in the middle of the night. But... maybe a few times a month, if I've had one of those nights where I just ate too much and I start feeling really panicked about it... I might go into the bathroom and just... make myself sick. \n\nIt's just that I work really hard to look put-together and professional on site, especially being a woman in construction, you know? You have to maintain a certain image. And I just get so scared of undoing all that effort. But it's really only a few times a month, so it's not a huge habit or anything. \n\nI know it probably doesn't sound great, but... I'm trying to be honest. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I hope I'm not sounding too messed up...\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself sick after eating, is it specifically because you feel you've eaten too much, or is it more about being worried about gaining weight or how your body looks?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's a little bit of both, but... mostly it's about the weight, honestly. \n\nI mean, I don't want to sound vain or anything, but since I got promoted and I'm up front at meetings more, I started wearing these more fitted work clothes.
